&\leq\|h^{(1)}_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}\|_{k_{1}^{\prime}\to k_{1}}\|h^{(2)}_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}\|_{k_{1}^{\prime}\to k_{2}}.\end{split}

Thus, we finish the proof.
∎

We conclude this subsection with a weighted estimate, whose proof can be found in
[
18
, Proposition 2.5]
. Also, see
[
20
, Proposition 2.9]
.

Proposition 2.19
.

Suppose that matrices
h=h_{kk^{\prime\prime}}
,
h^{(1)}=h^{(1)}_{kk^{\prime}}
and
h^{(2)}=h_{k^{\prime}k^{\prime\prime}}^{(2)}
satisfy that

h_{kk^{\prime\prime}}=\sum_{k^{\prime}}h_{kk^{\prime}}^{(1)}h_{k^{\prime}k^{\prime\prime}}^{(2)},

and
h_{kk^{\prime}}^{(1)}
is supported in
\{|k-k^{\prime}|\lesssim L\}
, then we have

\Big\|\Big(1+\frac{|k-k^{\prime\prime}|}{L}\Big)^{\kappa}h_{kk^{\prime\prime}}\Big\|_{\ell_{kk^{\prime\prime}}^{2}}\lesssim\|h^{(1)}_{kk^{\prime}}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\cdot\Big\|\Big(1+\frac{|k^{\prime}-k^{\prime\prime}|}{L}\Big)^{\kappa}h^{(2)}_{k^{\prime}k^{\prime\prime}}\Big\|_{\ell_{k^{\prime}k^{\prime\prime}}^{2}}.

2.4.
Random tensor estimates

In this subsection, we collect some probability results.
We start with the Wiener chaos estimate (see
[
39
, Theorem I.22]
).

Lemma 2.20
.

Let
\{g_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence of
independent standard real-valued Gaussian random variables. Given
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, let
\{P_{j}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence
of polynomials in
\bar{g}=\{g_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of degree at most

k
. Then, for
p\geq 2
, we have

\bigg\|\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}P_{j}(\bar{g})\bigg\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}\leq(p-1)^{\frac{k}{2}}\bigg\|\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}P_{j}(\bar{g})\bigg\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

We recall the definition of
A
-certain.

Definition 2.21
.

Let
\theta
be the constant as in Theorem
1.2
.
If some statement
S
for a random variable holds with probability
\mathbf{P}(S)\geq 1-C_{\theta}e^{-A^{\theta}}
for some
A>0
,
we say that this statement
S
is
A
-certain.

For a complex number
z
, we define
z^{+}=z
and
z^{-}=\bar{z}
.
For a finite index set
A
,
we also use the notation
z^{\zeta_{j}}
for

\{\zeta_{j}\}_{j\in A}
with
\zeta_{j}\in\{\pm\}
.

Definition 2.22
.

We say that
(k_{i},k_{j})
for
i,j\in A
is a pairing in
k_{A}
,
if
k_{i}=k_{j}
and
\zeta_{i}+\zeta_{j}=0
.
We say a pairing is over-paired if
k_{i}=k_{j}=k_{\ell}
for some
\ell\in A\backslash\{i,j\}
.

We also recall the following Large deviation inequality for multilinear Gaussians. It is a special case of
[
18
, Lemma 4.1]
. We give a simpler proof for completeness.

Lemma 2.23
.

Let
E\subset\mathbb{Z}
be a finite set, and
a=a_{k_{1}\cdots k_{r}}(\omega)
be a random tensor such that the collection
\{a_{k_{1}\cdots k_{r}}\}
is independent with the collection
\{g_{k}(\omega);k\in E\}
. Let

\zeta_{j}\in\{\pm\}
and assume that in the support of
a_{k_{1}\cdots k_{r}}
there is no pairing in
\{k_{1},\cdots,k_{r}\}
associated with the
signs
\zeta_{j}
. Define the random variable
X
as

\displaystyle X(\omega):=\sum_{k_{1},\cdots,k_{r}}a_{k_{1}\cdots k_{r}}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\eta_{k_{j}}(\omega)^{\zeta_{j}},

where
\eta_{k_{j}}\in\{g_{k_{j}},|g_{k_{j}}|^{2}-1\}
.
Then, for any
A>|E|
, we have
A
-certainly that

\displaystyle|X(\omega)|^{2}\leq A^{\theta}\cdot\sum_{k_{1},\cdots,k_{r}}|a_{k_{1}\cdots k_{r}}(\omega)|^{2}.

(2.56)

Proof.

By using Lemma
2.20
, we have

\mathbb{E}[|X|^{p}]\lesssim p^{\frac{2rp}{2}}\big(\mathbb{E}[|X|^{2}]\big)^{\frac{p}{2}}.

Due to the no-pairing assumption and independence, we have

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[|X|^{2}]&=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big|\sum_{k_{1},\cdots,k_{r}}a_{k_{1}\cdots k_{r}}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\eta_{k_{j}}(\omega)^{\zeta_{j}}\Big|^{2}\bigg]\\
&=\sum_{k_{1},\cdots,k_{r}}|a_{k_{1}\cdots k_{r}}|^{2}\prod_{j=1}^{r}\mathbb{E}\big[|\eta_{k_{j}}(\omega)|^{2}\big]\\
&\lesssim\sum_{k_{1},\cdots,k_{r}}|a_{k_{1}\cdots k_{r}}|^{2},\end{split}

where we used that
\mathbb{E}[|g_{k}|^{2}]\sim\mathbb{E}[|(|g_{k}|^{2}-1)|^{2}]\sim 1
.
Thus we have shown that

\mathbb{E}[|X|^{p}]\lesssim p^{\frac{2rp}{2}}\bigg(\sum_{k_{1},\cdots,k_{r}}|a_{k_{1}\cdots k_{r}}|^{2}\bigg)^{\frac{p}{2}}.

Then, (
2.56
) follows by a standard Nelson’s argument.
See, for example,
[
46
, Lemma 4.5]
.
∎

Now we are ready to state the random tensor estimate, whose proof can be found in
[
19
, Proposition 4.14]
. See also
[
20
, footnote 39]
and
[
9
]
for related discussions.

Proposition 2.24
.

Let
A
be a finite set and
h_{bck_{A}}=h_{bck_{A}}(\omega)
be a tensor, where each
k_{j}
and
(b,c)\in\mathbb{Z}^{q}
for
some integer
q\geq 2
. Given signs
\zeta_{j}\in\{\pm\}
, we also
assume that
\langle b-b_{0}\rangle,\langle c-c_{0}\rangle\lesssim M
and
\langle k_{j}\rangle\lesssim M
for some fixed
b_{0},c_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}
and all
j\in A
, where
M
is a dyadic number, and that in support of
h_{bck_{A}}

there is no pairing in
k_{A}
. Define the tensor

H_{bc}=\sum_{k_{A}}h_{bck_{A}}\prod_{j\in A}\eta^{\zeta_{j}}_{k_{j}},

(2.57)

where we restrict
k_{j}\in E
in (
2.57
),
E
being a finite set
such that
\{h_{bck_{A}}\}
is independent with
\{\eta_{k};k\in E\}
, and
\eta_{k_{j}}\in\{g_{k_{j}},|g_{k_{j}}|^{2}-1\}
.
Then
T^{-1}M
-certainly, we have

\|H_{bc}\|_{b\to c}\lesssim T^{-\theta}M^{\theta}\cdot\max_{(B,C)}\|h\|_{bk_{B}\to ck_{C}},

for some
T\ll 1
,
where
(B,C)
runs over all partitions of
A
.

Remark 2.25
.

In what follows, we may ignore the constant
T^{-\theta}M^{\theta}
, as these constants may be absorbed by choosing
\theta\ll 1
in our analysis.
For instance, the factor
T^{-\theta}
can be absorbed by Proposition
2.2
by choosing
\theta\ll b_{1}-b
.
See also
[
19
,
20
]
for similar arguments.

3.
Proof of Theorem
1.2

We are ready to prove the main result of this paper, i.e. Theorem
1.2
.
As explained in Subsection
1.4.3
, we shall proceed with an induction argument.
To be more precise,
to construct the solution
u(t)
to (
1.1
) with the formal expansion (
1.43
), we need to (i) construct
z_{N}
and
\mathfrak{h}^{N,L}
with
1\leq L<N^{1-\delta}
for each dyadic
N\geq 1
; (ii) show the convergence of (
1.43
) in a proper space. Now we illustrate how to construct
z_{N}
and
\mathfrak{h}^{N,L}
in an induction manner.
We first note that
\Pi_{1/2}=0
3
3

3

Here we used the fact that
\{k\in\mathbb{Z};\langle k\rangle\leq 1/2\}=\emptyset
.
, which implies that
u_{1/2}=y_{1/2}=F_{1/2}=z_{1/2}=0
,
\mathcal{H}^{N,1/2}=\Pi_{N}
, and
\psi_{N,1/2}=\chi(t)F_{N}
.
Then we move to
z_{1}
.
We note that from (
1.29
) that

\displaystyle y_{1}=\psi_{1,1/2}+z_{1}=F_{1}+z_{1}.

(3.1)

From (
1.30
) and (
3.1
), it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}z_{1}(t)&=-i\chi(t)\bigg(\int^{t}_{0}\Pi_{1}\mathcal{M}(y_{1},y_{1},y_{1})(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}+\int^{t}_{0}\Pi_{1}\mathcal{R}(y_{1})(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}\bigg)\\
&=-i\chi(t)\sum_{w_{1}^{(i)}\in\{F_{1},z_{1}\}}\bigg(\int^{t}_{0}\Pi_{1}\mathcal{M}(w_{1}^{(1)},w_{1}^{(2)},w_{1}^{(3)})(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}\\
&\hphantom{XXX}+\int^{t}_{0}\Pi_{1}\mathcal{R}(w_{1}^{(1)},w_{1}^{(2)},w_{1}^{(3)})(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}\bigg)\end{split}

(3.2)

which can be solved, provided
T\ll 1
, as
\Pi_{1}
is the projection to zero frequency.
Given
z_{1}
solved from (
3.2
), we obtain
y_{1}
, from (
1.29
) and
\psi_{1,1/2}=F_{1}
, and thus
u_{1}
by (
1.24
).
Next, we can use (
1.28
) to construct
\psi_{N,1}
, which is a linear equation that can be solved locally for small
t
4
4

4

See (
1.28
) for more details.
, where we used
u_{1}
that we obtained earlier.
By plugging
\psi_{2,1}
and
y_{1}
into (
1.30
), we can solve for
z_{2}
5
5

5

This can be done via a fixed point argument as it is essentially an ODE system. Also see Section
5
for further discussion.
.
Similarly, we can get
y_{2}
from (
1.29
) and then
u_{2}
from (
1.24
).
We repeat the above process to construct
\psi_{N,2}
using (
1.28
) and
u_{2}
.
By iterating this procedure, we obtain a sequence of
\{z_{L},u_{L},\psi_{N,L}\}
for all dyadic numbers
L,N
, such that
L<N^{1-\delta}
. To show point (ii), which is the convergence of (
1.43
) in some space, we need to show that each term in the sequence
\{z_{L},u_{L},\psi_{N,L}\}
satisfies some proper bounds, which are elaborated in the following definition.

Definition 3.1
.

Given
0<T\ll 1
,
b>\frac{1}{2}
sufficiently closed to
\frac{1}{2}
, and let
J=[-T,T]
and
\chi(t)=\eta_{T}(t)
. For any
dyadic number
M
, consider the following statements, which we call

\text{{{{{Local}}\/}}}(M):

(i)

For the operator
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}
defined in (
1.40
),
where
L<\min(M,N^{1-\delta})
, we have

\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}\|_{Y^{b}}\leq L^{-\delta_{0}},\text{\hskip 10.22217pt}\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}\|_{Z^{b}}\leq N^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}},

(3.3)

as well as

\Big\|\Big(1+\frac{|k-k^{\prime}|}{L}\Big)^{\kappa}h_{kk^{\prime}}^{N,L}\Big\|_{Z^{b}}\leq{{N}}.

(3.4)

Here the
Y^{b}
and
Z^{b}
norms are defined in (
2.3
) and (
2.4
), respectively.

(ii)

For the terms
z_{N}
, where
N\leq M
, we have

\|z_{N}\|_{X^{b}(J)}\leq N^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}.

(3.5)

(iii)

For any dyadic numbers
L_{1}
,
L_{2}<M\leq N
the operator

\mathcal{P}^{\pm}
defined by

\begin{split}&\mathcal{P}^{+}[\psi](t)=\mathcal{P}^{+}_{N,L_{1},L_{2}}[\psi](t):=-i\chi(t)\int^{t}_{0}\Pi_{N}\mathcal{M}(y_{L_{1}},y_{L_{2}},\psi)(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}\\
&\mathcal{P}^{-}[\psi](t)=\mathcal{P}^{-}_{N,L_{1},L_{2}}[\psi](t):=-i\chi(t)\int^{t}_{0}\Pi_{N}\mathcal{M}(y_{L_{1}},\psi,y_{L_{2}})(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}\end{split}

(3.6)

whose kernel
P_{kk^{\prime}}^{\pm}(t,t^{\prime})
has Fourier transform
\widehat{P_{kk^{\prime}}^{\pm}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})
, which satisfies
|k-k^{\prime}|\lesssim L_{\max}
and

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2(1-b)}\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle^{-2b}\|\widehat{P^{\pm}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}^{2}d\tau d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim T^{2\theta}L^{-6\delta_{0}}_{\max},

(3.7)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2(1-b)}\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle^{-2b}\|\widehat{P^{\pm}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\|_{kk^{\prime}}^{2}d\tau d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim T^{2\theta}N^{1+\gamma_{0}}L^{-1-4\gamma_{0}}_{\max},

(3.8)

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2(1-b)}\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle^{-2b}\Big\|\Big(1+\frac{|k-k^{\prime}|}{{{L_{\max}}}}\Big)^{\kappa}\widehat{P^{\pm}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\Big\|_{kk^{\prime}}^{2}d\tau d\tau^{\prime}\lesssim T^{2\theta}N^{2},

(3.9)

where
L_{\max}=\max(L_{1},L_{2})
.

In the above definition, we choose the parameters
0<\varepsilon\ll\delta\ll\gamma_{0}\ll\gamma\ll\delta_{0}\ll\alpha-1
and
\kappa\gg 1
.

Remark 3.2
.

Random averaging operators
\mathcal{P}^{\pm}
play a crucial role in our later analysis. These operators were first introduced in
[
18
]
for Schrödinger equations; see Section
4
for further details. In
[
18
, Proposition 3.3]
, it is observed that the operator
\mathcal{P}^{+}
enjoys a better bound.
A similar better estimate for the
\mathcal{P}^{+}
than (
3.8
) also holds; see Section
4
for details. However, this improved estimate for
\mathcal{P}^{+}
does not play an essential role in this paper.

Now, by using Definition
3.1
, we are ready to state the main a priori estimate, which is the key ingredient in proving Theorem
1.2
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Given
0<T\ll 1
,
the probability that
\text{{{{{Local}}\/}}}(M/2)
holds but
\text{{{{{Local}}\/}}}(M)
does not hold is less than
C_{\theta}e^{-(T^{-1}M)^{\theta}}
for some
\theta,C_{\theta}>0
.

The proof of Proposition
3.3
will occupy Section
4
and Section
5
.

3.1.
Proof of the main theorem

Let us prove Theorem
1.2
by assuming Proposition
3.3
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

From Proposition
3.3
, it follows that
T^{-1}
-certainly, the event
\text{{{Local}}}(M)
, defined in Definition
3.1
, holds for any
M
.
Recall the decomposition (
1.42
) that

\displaystyle\begin{split}y_{N}&=\chi(t)F_{N}+\sum_{1\leq L\leq L_{N}}\mathfrak{h}^{N,L}[\chi(\cdot)F_{N}]+z_{N}(t)\\
&=\chi(t)F_{N}+\sum_{1\leq L\leq L_{N}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}[F_{N}]+z_{N}(t)\end{split}

(3.10)

where
F_{N}
is given in (
1.26
),
\mathfrak{h}^{N,L}
and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}
are the operators given in (
1.37
) and (
1.40
), and
z_{N}
is the solution to (
1.30
).
From the definition of
\text{{{Local}}}(M)
in Definition
3.1
, we see that for each
N
, the decomposition of
y_{N}
in the above is well defined, i.e. both
\mathfrak{h}^{N,L}
and
z_{N}
are well defined.
To prove the local well-posedness part of Theorem
1.2
, it suffices to justify the convergence of the summation
\sum y_{N}
in some proper sense.

From (
1.8
) and (
1.26
), we see that

\displaystyle u_{0}^{\omega}=\sum_{N\geq 1}F_{N}\quad\textup{ converges in }H^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2}-}(\mathbb{T}).

(3.11)

From (
1.41
),
we also have that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}[F_{N}]=\sum_{k}e^{ik\cdot x}\sum_{k^{\prime}}h_{kk^{\prime}}^{N,L}(t)\frac{g_{k^{\prime}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

(3.12)

where
h_{kk^{\prime}}^{N,L}
, the kernel of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}
given in (
1.41
), is independent of
\{g_{k^{\prime}}\}_{N/2<\langle{k^{\prime}}\rangle\leq N}
.
Therefore, by using
Proposition
2.24
(as well as Remark
2.25
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}[F_{N}]\|_{X^{b}}^{2}&=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2b}\bigg\|\sum_{k^{\prime}}\widehat{h_{kk^{\prime}}^{N,L}}(\tau)\frac{g_{k^{\prime}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\bigg\|_{\ell_{k}^{2}}^{2}d\tau\\
&\lesssim N^{-\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2b}\big\|\widehat{h_{kk^{\prime}}^{N,L}}(\tau)\big\|_{kk^{\prime}}^{2}d\tau\\
&=N^{-\alpha}\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}\|^{2}_{Z^{b}(J)}\\
&\lesssim N^{1-\alpha+2\gamma_{0}}L^{-1},\end{split}

(3.13)

where we used (
3.3
) in the last step.
Then from (
3.4
), by choosing
\kappa\gg 1
large enough, we have
|k-k^{\prime}|\leq LN^{\varepsilon}
for some
\varepsilon\ll 1
. This together with (
3.12
) implies that the Fourier support of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}[F_{N}]
is
N/4<|k|\leq 2N
. Therefore, from (
3.13
) we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bigg\|\sum_{1\leq L\leq L_{N}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}[F_{N}]\bigg\|_{C^{0}_{J}H^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2}-\gamma}_{x}}&\lesssim N^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2}-\gamma}\bigg\|\sum_{1\leq L\leq L_{N}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}[F_{N}]\bigg\|_{X^{b}(J)}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2}-\gamma}\sum_{1\leq L\leq L_{N}}\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}[F_{N}]\|_{X^{b}(J)}\\
&\lesssim N^{\gamma_{0}-\gamma},\end{split}

where we used the fact that
b>\frac{1}{2}
. Also, recalling that
\gamma_{0}\ll\gamma
, we thus conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{N\geq 1}\sum_{1\leq L\leq L_{N}}\mathfrak{h}^{N,L}[\chi(\cdot)F_{N}]\quad\textup{ converges in }C(J;H^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2}-\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))\subset C(J;L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))

(3.14)

by choosing
\gamma\ll 1
.

From (
3.5
), we see that

\displaystyle\sum_{N\geq 1}z_{N}\quad\textup{ converges in }X^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma-,b}(J),

(3.15)

and thus in
C(J;H^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma-}(\mathbb{T}))
for some short time interval
J
.

Finally, by collecting (
3.10
), (
3.11
), (
3.14
), and (
3.15
), we conclude that the sequence

u_{N}=\sum_{M=1}^{N}y_{M}

converges in
C(J;H^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2}-\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))\subset C(J;L^{2}(\mathbb{T}))
; i.e. there exists an unique
u\in C(J;H^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2}-\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
such that

u=\lim_{N}u_{N}=\lim_{N}\sum_{M=1}^{N}y_{M}

converges in
C(J;H^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2}-\gamma}(\mathbb{T}))
.
∎

Remark 3.4
.

The rigorous proof of (
3.13
) would require a
meshing argument
.
As the application of meshing argument has become a standard procedure nowadays, we only sketch its key ideas here.
For detailed argument and further discussion, see
[
18
, Remark 4.3]
,
[
19
, Subsection 6.1]
, and
[
20
, Subsection 3.4]
.
First, we may restrict to low modulation
|\tau|\leq N^{\kappa}
for some
\kappa\gg 1
, as otherwise, we may gain considerable smoothing. Second, we divide the big interval
\{\tau\in\mathbb{R};|\tau|\leq N^{\kappa}\}
into small intervals of length
\nu:=\exp(-(\log N)^{6})
.
Third, by taking averages
on these small intervals and using Poincaré inequality,
we can approximate the tensor
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}=(\widehat{h^{N,L}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau))_{k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}
by
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}_{\text{avg}}^{N,L}=(\widehat{(h^{N,L}_{\text{avg}})_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau))_{k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}}
in the sense that

\|\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{N,L}_{\text{avg}}\|_{Z^{b}(J)}\lesssim\,\nu\,\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2b}\|\widehat{h^{N,L}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau)\|_{kk^{\prime}}^{2}d\tau+\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2b}\|\partial_{\tau}\widehat{h^{N,L}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau)\|_{kk^{\prime}}^{2}d\tau\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Once the above is properly controlled, it suffices to consider at most
\exp(\kappa(\log N)^{6})
different values of
\tau
. This, in particular, allows us to obtain (
3.13
) after removing an exceptional set whose probability is still
O(e^{-(T^{-1}M)^{\theta}})
.
Roughly speaking, we may fix the
\tau
variable in the computation such as (
3.13
).
Similar meshing arguments would be needed when we reduce to (
4.7
), (
4.11
), (
4.12
), (
4.13
), and (
5.9
), from their time-dependent counterpart.

3.2.
Reformulation

It remains to prove Proposition
3.3
.
As explained at the beginning of this section, the main strategy is to proceed via induction.
Recall that
\Pi_{1/2}=0
, and thus
u_{1/2}=y_{1/2}=F_{1/2}=z_{1/2}=0
,
\mathcal{H}^{N,1/2}=\Pi_{N}
, and
\psi_{N,1/2}=\chi(t)F_{N}
.
Therefore,
\text{{{Local}}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)
holds trivially.
Here and in what follows,
\text{{{Local}}}\left(M\right)
is given in Definition
3.1
.

In what follows, let us assume
\text{{{Local}}}\left(M^{\prime}\right)
holds for all
M^{\prime}\leq\frac{M}{2}
for some
M\geq 1
.
Then, the aim is to show that
\text{{{Local}}}\left({M}\right)
holds with large probability; i.e. the probability of
\textup{{{{{Local}}\/}}}(M)
does not hold is less than
C_{\theta}e^{-(T^{-1}M)^{\theta}}
for some
\theta,C_{\theta}>0
.

Before proceeding to show
\text{{{Local}}}\left({M}\right)
, let us reformulate the estimates given in
\text{{{Local}}}\left(\frac{M}{2}\right)
.
From
\text{{{Local}}}\left(\frac{M}{2}\right)
, we have (
3.3
), (
3.4
), and (
3.5
) with
N\leq\frac{M}{2}
and
L<\min(\frac{M}{2},N^{1-\delta})
. This together with (
1.42
) implies that

\displaystyle y_{L}(t)=\chi(t)F_{L}+\sum_{1\leq R<L^{1-\delta}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{L,R}[F_{L}](t)+z_{L}(t),

(3.16)

is well defined for all
L\leq\frac{M}{2}
and
t\in J=[-T,T]
.
To show that
\text{{{Local}}}\left({M}\right)
-(ii) holds, that is, (
3.5
), we substitute
y_{L}
for
L\leq\frac{M}{2}
from (
3.16
) into (
1.30
) and then solve for
z_{M}
. Likewise, to show that
\text{{{Local}}}\left({M}\right)
-(iii) holds, that is, (
3.7
), (
3.8
), and (
3.9
) with
L_{1},L_{2}<N\leq M
, we substitute
y_{L}
from (
3.16
) into the random averaging operators
\mathcal{P}^{\pm}
in (
3.6
). To simplify the notations, we can assume that
y_{L}
is either
\chi(t)F_{L}
,
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{L,R}(F_{L})
, or
z_{L}
, depending on the decomposition (
3.16
).
In particular, we assume the input
w_{L}
is one of the following three types:

•

Type (G), we have

({w_{L}})_{k}(t):=\mathbf{1}_{L/2<\langle k\rangle\leq L}\frac{g_{k}(\omega)}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha/2}}{\chi}(t).

•

Type (C), where

({w_{L}})_{k}(t):=\sum_{k^{\prime}}{h_{kk^{\prime}}^{L,R}}(t)\frac{g_{k^{\prime}}(\omega)}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha/2}},

(3.17)

with
h_{kk^{\prime}}^{L,R}
being
supported in the set
\{(k,k^{\prime});L/2<\langle k^{\prime}\rangle\leq L\}
,
\mathcal{B}_{\leq R}
-measurable for some

R<L^{1-\delta}
, and satisfying the bounds

\begin{split}&\|\langle\tau\rangle^{b}\widehat{h_{kk^{\prime}}^{L,R}}(\tau)\|_{L_{\tau}^{2}(\ell_{k}^{2}\to\ell_{k^{\prime}}^{2})}\lesssim R^{-\delta_{0}},\\
&\|\langle\tau\rangle^{b}\widehat{h_{kk^{\prime}}^{L,R}}(\tau)\|_{L_{\tau}^{2}\ell_{kk^{\prime}}^{2}}\lesssim L^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}R^{-\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

(3.18)

for some
b>\frac{1}{2}
.
Moreover, from
(
3.4
) we may assume that
h_{kk^{\prime}}^{L,R}
is supported in {
|k-k^{\prime}|\lesssim L^{\varepsilon}R
} for
\varepsilon\ll 1
that only depends on
\kappa
.

•

Type (D), where
(w_{L})_{k}
is supported in
\{k\in\mathbb{Z};\langle k\rangle\leq L\}
and satisfies

\|w_{L}\|_{X^{b}}\lesssim L^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma},

(3.19)

for some
b>\frac{1}{2}
, provided
1\leq L\leq\frac{M}{2}
.

Remark 3.5
.

By letting
h^{L,1/2}_{kk^{\prime}}:=\mathbf{1}_{L/2<\langle k\rangle\leq L}\mathbf{1}_{k=k^{\prime}}\cdot{\chi}(t)
,
we can view type (G) input as a special case of type (C).
Therefore, we will only consider type (C) and type (D) in what follows.

3.3.
Unitary property

In this subsection, we prove a crucial cancellation property of
\mathcal{H}^{N,L}
, originated from the
L^{2}
conservation of the linear equation (
1.31
).
We first show that the operator
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}
given in (
1.39
) is well defined, provided

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}^{N,L}\big\|_{Y^{b,b}}<1.

(3.20)

Here the operator norm
Y^{b,b}
is given in (
2.5
).
From (
1.35
) and (
3.20
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}[\Psi_{0}]\|_{X^{b}}&=\|\mathcal{H}^{N,L}[\chi(\cdot)\Psi_{0}]\|_{X^{b}}=\|(1-\mathcal{P}^{N,L})^{-1}[\chi(\cdot)\Psi_{0}]\|_{X^{b}}\\
&\leq\|\chi(\cdot)\Psi_{0}\|_{X^{b}}+\sum_{n\geq 1}\|(\mathcal{P}^{N,L})^{n}[\chi(\cdot)\Psi_{0}]\|_{X^{b}}\\
&\leq\|\chi(\cdot)\Psi_{0}\|_{X^{b}}+\sum_{n\geq 1}\|\mathcal{P}^{N,L}\|_{Y^{b,b}}^{n}\|\chi(\cdot)\Psi_{0}]\|_{X^{b}},\end{split}

which implies that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}
is well defined, provided (
3.20
).
Since
b>\frac{1}{2}
, by the Sobolev embedding, we have that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}[\Psi_{0}]\in C_{T}L^{2}_{x}
.
We have the following group property on
[-T,T]
, i.e.

\Psi(t_{2},\Psi(t_{1}))=\Psi(t_{1}+t_{2},\Psi(0)),

where
\Psi(t,W)
is the solution to (
1.32
) with initial data
\Psi_{0}=W
.
Thus
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}(t)
is invertible since
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}(t)\circ\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}(-t)={\rm Id}
.
Finally, we note that (
3.20
) is guaranteed by (
3.7
).
As a consequence, we also have the following unitary property.

Lemma 3.6
.

Given
M>\frac{1}{2}
, assume
\text{{{{{Local}}\/}}}(M)
given in Definition
3.1
holds.
For
L<\min(M,N^{1-\delta})
, there exists
T\ll 1
such that for each
|t|\leq T
, the matrix

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}(t)=\{\widetilde{H}_{kk^{\prime}}^{N,L}(t)\}_{kk^{\prime}}
is unitary, i.e.

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\widetilde{H}_{k_{1}k}^{N,L}(t)\overline{\widetilde{H}^{N,L}_{k_{2}k}}(t)d\tau=\delta_{k_{1}}(k_{2})

(3.21)

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}
is the linear mapping defined in
(
1.39
).

Bourgain
[
4
]
first observed this property in the context of Hartree-type NLS.
See also
[
20
]
.
Here we give a proof for reader’s convenience.

Proof.

From (
1.31
), we know that

\partial_{t}\sum_{k}|\Psi_{k}|^{2}=2\sum_{k}\operatorname{Re}(\overline{\Psi_{k}}\partial_{t}\Psi_{k})=4\operatorname{Im}\bigg(\sum_{k}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}-k_{2}+k_{3}=k\\
k_{2}\not\in\{k_{1},k_{3}\}\end{subarray}}e^{it^{\prime}\Phi}\cdot(u_{L})_{k_{1}}\overline{(u_{L})_{k_{2}}}\overline{\Psi_{k}}\Psi_{k_{3}}\bigg).

By swapping
(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\mapsto(k_{3},k_{2},k_{1},k)
in the summand,
we see that we are taking imaginary part for reals, which gives zero. Namely

\partial_{t}\sum_{k}|\Psi_{k}|^{2}=0,

for
|t|\leq T
.
It then follows that

\|\Psi(t)\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2}=\|\Psi_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{T})}^{2},\text{\quad for all }|t|\leq T.

This shows that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}(t)
is isometric on
L^{2}
for
t\in[-T,T]
.
Furthermore, given
t\in[-T,T]
, we know that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}(t)
is invertible and thus surjective. Therefore, for fixed
t\in[-T,T]
, the linear operator
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}(t)
is unitary
6
6

6

A linear map is unitary if it is surjective and isometric.
, i.e.

\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}(t)\big)^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}(t)=\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}(t)\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}(t)\big)^{*}=\text{Id},

for
|t|\leq T
, which finishes the proof.
∎

We have the following corollary of Lemma
3.6
.

Corollary 3.7
.

With the same assumption as in Lemma
3.6
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k}\sum_{L_{1},L_{2}\leq L}h^{N,L_{1}}_{k_{1}k}(t)\overline{h^{N,L_{2}}_{k_{2}k}(t)}\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}=\sum_{k}\sum_{L_{1},L_{2}\leq L}h^{N,L_{1}}_{k_{1}k}(t)\overline{h^{N,L_{2}}_{k_{2}k}(t)}\bigg(\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}-\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\bigg)

(3.22)

provided
|t|\leq T
and
k_{1}\neq k_{2}
.

Proof.

From (
1.41
), we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{L_{i}\leq L}h_{k_{i}k}^{N,L_{i}}(t)=\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}_{k_{i}k}(t),

(3.23)

for
i=1,2
.
Then it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{L_{1},L_{2}\leq L}\sum_{k}h_{k_{1}k}^{N,L_{1}}(t)\overline{h_{k_{2}k}^{N,L_{2}}}(t)=\sum_{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}_{k_{1}k}(t)\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{N,L}_{k_{2}k}(t)=\delta_{k_{1}}(k_{2})\end{split}

for
|t|\leq T
. Thus (
3.22
) follows.
∎

Remark 3.8
.

Without loss of generality, we may assume that

\sum_{k}h^{N,L_{1}}_{k_{1}k}(t)\overline{h^{N,L_{2}}_{k_{2}k}(t)}\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}=\sum_{k}h^{N,L_{1}}_{k_{1}k}(t)\overline{h^{N,L_{2}}_{k_{2}k}(t)}\bigg(\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}-\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\bigg),

in our later analysis. The reason is that from Corollary
3.7
, we can always add

0=\sum_{k}\sum_{L_{1},L_{2}\leq L}h^{N,L_{1}}_{k_{1}k}(t)\overline{h^{N,L_{2}}_{k_{2}k}(t)}\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

from the beginning of our analysis.

4.
The random averaging operators

This and the next section will be devoted to the proof of Proposition
3.3
.
The plan of the proof is as follows;
we first further reduce the problem to a time-dependent problem in Subsection
4.1
;
then we prove (
3.7
)–(
3.9
) of
\text{{{Local}}}\left(M\right)
in Subsections
4.2
and
4.3
; in Subsection
4.4
,
we verify (
3.3
) and (
3.4
) of
\text{{{Local}}}\left(M\right)
.
We defer the proof of (
3.5
) to the next section.

4.1.
Further reduction

To make the proof more clear, we can recast the estimates (
3.7
) – (
3.9
) in a simpler way. The key point is that the main solution space
X^{b}
for
b>\frac{1}{2}
embeds in
C_{t}
in the temporal variable, so we can ignore the
t
variable in most of our analysis.

Recall operators
\mathcal{P}^{\pm}
defined in (
3.6
). Due to the factor
\chi(t)=\eta(T^{-1}t)
, by Proposition

2.2
, we can gain a small factor
T^{\theta}
.
Recall that
\widehat{P^{\pm}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})
is the temporal Fourier transform of the kernel
P^{\pm}_{kk^{\prime}}(t,t^{\prime})
of
\mathcal{P}^{\pm}
in (
3.6
), i.e.

\widehat{(\mathcal{P}^{\pm}\psi)_{k}}(\tau)=\sum_{k^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\widehat{P^{\pm}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\widehat{\psi_{k^{\prime}}}(\tau^{\prime})d\tau^{\prime}.

We decompose
\{|k^{\prime}|\sim N_{3}\}
into intervals
B^{\prime}_{i}
of lengths

L_{\max}=\max(L_{1},L_{2})
, and then write

\widehat{P^{\pm}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})=\sum_{i}\widehat{P^{\pm}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\mathbf{1}_{k^{\prime}\in B_{i}^{\prime}}.

Here, we may further restrict to the low modulation case
||k|^{\alpha}-|k^{\prime}|^{\alpha}|\lesssim L_{\max}^{\kappa}
for some
\kappa\gg 1
, as otherwise, we may gain considerable regularity from an
X^{s,b}
argument. With this further restriction, we note that the number of intervals
B_{i}^{\prime}
is at most of
O(L_{\max}^{c\kappa})
.
See
[
19
, Proposition 4.15]
for a similar argument.
Due to the fact that
|k-k^{\prime}|\lesssim L_{\max}
, we
see that

\|\widehat{P^{\pm}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\lesssim\sup_{i}\|\widehat{P^{\pm}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\mathbf{1}_{k^{\prime}\in B_{i}^{\prime}}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}.

(4.1)

Therefore,
in what follows, we may assume that
k
and
k^{\prime}
are both localized in intervals of length
L_{\max}=\max(L_{1},L_{2})
.

We start with the operator
\mathcal{P}^{+}
.
By using (
1.22
), (
2.8
), and
(
3.6
), we may rewrite the temporal
Fourier transform of the kernel as

\begin{split}&\widehat{P^{+}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\\
&\quad=-i\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}-k_{2}=k-k^{\prime}\\
k_{2}\not\in\{k_{1},k^{\prime}\}\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathcal{K}(\tau,\Phi+\tau^{\prime}+\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\cdot(\widehat{y_{L_{1}}})_{k_{1}}(\tau_{1})\cdot\overline{(\widehat{y_{L_{2}}})_{k_{2}}}(\tau_{2})d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2}\\
&\quad=-i\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}\mathcal{K}(\tau,m+\Phi-[\Phi]+\tau^{\prime}+\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt\times\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\cdot(\widehat{y_{L_{1}}})_{k_{1}}(\tau_{1})\cdot\overline{(\widehat{y_{L_{2}}})_{k_{2}}}(\tau_{2})d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2},\end{split}

(4.2)

where
\Phi
is defined in (
1.23
),
[\Phi]
is the largest integer
that does not greater than
\Phi
, and the base tensor
{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}
is given by
(
2.44
). Then by Minkowski inequality, (
4.2
), and (
2.9
), we have

\begin{split}\|\mathcal{P}^{+}&\|_{Y^{1-b,b^{\prime}}}^{2}\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2(1-b)}\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle^{-2b^{\prime}}\|\widehat{P^{+}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}^{2}d\tau d\tau^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{-2b}\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle^{-2b^{\prime}}\bigg(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\langle\tau_{1}\rangle^{-b}\langle\tau_{2}\rangle^{-b}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\Big\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}\langle\tau-m+\Phi-[\Phi]-\tau^{\prime}-\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}\rangle^{-1}\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt\times\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\cdot(\langle\tau_{1}\rangle^{b}\widehat{y_{L_{1}}})_{k_{1}}(\tau_{1})\cdot\overline{(\langle\tau_{2}\rangle^{b}\widehat{y_{L_{2}}})_{k_{2}}}(\tau_{2})\Big\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2}\bigg)^{2}d\tau d\tau^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{-2b}\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle^{-2b^{\prime}}\bigg(\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\langle\tau-m-\tau^{\prime}-\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}\rangle^{-1}\langle\tau_{1}\rangle^{-b}\langle\tau_{2}\rangle^{-b}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt\times\Big\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\cdot(\langle\tau_{1}\rangle^{b}\widehat{y_{L_{1}}})_{k_{1}}(\tau_{1})\cdot\overline{(\langle\tau_{2}\rangle^{b}\widehat{y_{L_{2}}})_{k_{2}}}(\tau_{2})\Big\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2}\bigg)^{2}d\tau d\tau^{\prime},\end{split}

(4.3)

where
b>b^{\prime}>\frac{1}{2}
.
To deal with the summation over
m
in (
4.3
), we need to localize the
frequencies
k,k_{1},k_{2},k^{\prime}
. To be more precise, if
m=[\Phi]\in\mathbb{Z}
ranges over an interval of size
R
, then from our construction,
we have

\sum_{m}\langle\tau-m-\tau^{\prime}-\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}\rangle^{-1}\lesssim\log(1+R).

(4.4)

In what follows, we shall use some special cases of (
4.4
). Recall

\Phi=|{k_{1}}|^{\alpha}-|{k_{2}}|^{\alpha}+|k^{\prime}|^{\alpha}-|{k}|^{\alpha}
from (
1.23
) and
the relation
k=k_{1}-k_{2}+k^{\prime}
. If we assume
|k_{1}|\lesssim L_{1}
,
|k_{2}|\lesssim L_{2}
, and
k\in B_{i}^{\prime}
, where
B_{i}^{\prime}
is the ball considered in
(
4.1
), then we have a refined bound

\sum_{m}\langle\tau-m-\tau^{\prime}-\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}\rangle^{-1}\lesssim\log(1+L_{\max}).

(4.5)

By using (
4.5
) and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in
\tau_{1},\tau_{2}
integrations, we may bound (
4.3
) by

(\log(1+L_{\max}))^{2}\Big\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\cdot(\langle\tau_{1}\rangle^{b}\widehat{y_{L_{1}}})_{k_{1}}(\tau_{1})\cdot\overline{(\langle\tau_{2}\rangle^{b}\widehat{y_{L_{2}}})_{k_{2}}}(\tau_{2})\Big\|^{2}_{L_{\tau_{1}\tau_{2}}^{2}(k\to k^{\prime})},

(4.6)

where we used that
b,b^{\prime}>\frac{1}{2}
.
From the discussion in Subsection
3.2
, we proceed with estimating (
4.6
) by replacing
y_{L_{j}}
with an input
w_{L_{j}}
of either type (C) or type (D), respectively.

From the definition of type (C), type (D),
Remark
3.4
,
and (
4.6
), we may further simplify the estimate (
3.7
) to a time-independent estimate.
To be more precise, to prove (
3.7
),
it suffices to show

\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\lesssim L^{-4\delta_{0}}_{\max},

(4.7)

where
\mathscr{Y}^{+}_{k,k^{\prime}}
is the time-dependent random tensor given by

\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\cdot(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\overline{(w_{L_{2}})_{k_{2}}}.

(4.8)

Here
w_{L_{j}}
for
j\in\{1,2\}
are of the following two types, with a
slight abuse of notation, we still call them type (C) and type (D).

•

Type (C), where

(w_{L_{j}})_{k_{j}}:=\sum_{{k_{j}^{\prime}}}h_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}(\omega)\frac{g_{k_{j}^{\prime}}(\omega)}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{j}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha/2}},

with
h_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}
supported in the set
\{k_{j}^{\prime};L_{j}/2<\langle k_{j}^{\prime}\rangle\leq L_{j}\}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\leq R_{j}}
-measurable for some
R_{j}\leq L_{j}^{1-\delta}
, and satisfying
the bounds

\begin{split}&\|h_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}\|_{\ell_{k_{j}}^{2}\to\ell_{k_{j}^{\prime}}^{2}}\lesssim R_{j}^{-\delta_{0}},\\
&\|h_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}\|_{{\ell_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{2}}}\lesssim L_{j}^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}R_{j}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

(4.9)

for
0<\delta\ll\gamma_{0}\ll\gamma\ll\delta_{0}\ll\alpha-1
. Moreover, from
(
3.4
) we may assume that
h_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}
is supported in
{
|k_{j}-k_{j}^{\prime}|\lesssim L_{j}^{\varepsilon}R_{j}
} for any
\varepsilon>0
.

•

Type (D), where
(w_{L_{j}})_{k_{j}}
is supported in
\{k_{j}\in\mathbb{Z};\langle k_{j}\rangle\leq L_{j}\}
, and satisfies

\|(w_{L_{j}})_{k_{j}}\|_{\ell_{k_{j}}^{2}}\lesssim L_{j}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}.

(4.10)

A similar argument allows us to reduce (
3.8
) to

\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{kk^{\prime}}\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\gamma_{0}}{3}}L^{{{-\frac{1}{2}-3\gamma_{0}}}}_{\max},

(4.11)

where
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}
is as in (
4.8
) and
0<\varepsilon^{\prime}\ll 1
.

The same argument as above also works for the estimate (
3.7
) with the operator
\mathcal{P}^{-}
, for which we only need to prove the following time-independent random matrix estimates.

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{-4\delta_{0}}_{\max},

(4.12)

\displaystyle\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}\|_{kk^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\gamma_{0}}{3}}L^{{{-\frac{1}{2}-3\gamma_{0}}}}_{\max},

(4.13)

where the corresponding random tensor
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}
is given by

\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}}\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\cdot(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\left({w_{L_{3}}}\right)_{k_{3}}.

(4.14)

Here
w_{L_{1}}
and
w_{L_{3}}
in (
4.14
) are of either type (C) satisfying (
4.9
), or type (D) satisfying (
4.10
).

The proof of (
4.7
), (
4.11
), (
4.12
), and (
4.13
) will be detailed in Subsections
4.2
and
4.3
.
The above argument yields the following conditional proof of (
3.7
) – (
3.9
) in
\textup{{{Local}}}\left({M}\right)
.

Proof of (
3.7
) – (
3.9
) in
\textup{{{Local}}}\left({M}\right)
.

Given
\text{{{Local}}}\left(\frac{M}{2}\right)
, we may decompose
y_{L_{i}}
into
w_{L_{i}}
of type (C) or type (D) for
L_{i}\leq\frac{M}{2}
.
From the above argument,
we have seen that (
3.7
) and (
3.8
) follows from (
4.7
), (
4.11
), (
4.12
), and (
4.13
).
Finally, the estimate (
3.9
) follows from (
3.8
) and the fact
|k-k^{\prime}|\lesssim L_{\max}
.
∎

We conclude this subsection by recording a consequence of (
3.7
) – (
3.9
).

Corollary 4.1
.

Given
\textup{{{Local}}}\left({M}\right)
defined in Definition
3.1
and the above notations, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}^{\pm}\|_{Y^{b,b}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{c\theta}L_{\max}^{-2\delta_{0}},

(4.15)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}^{\pm}\|_{Z^{b,b}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{c\theta}N^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2\gamma_{0}}{3}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}_{\max},

(4.16)

\displaystyle\bigg\|\bigg(1+\frac{|k-k^{\prime}|}{L_{\max}}\bigg)^{\kappa}\widehat{P_{kk^{\prime}}^{\pm}}\bigg\|_{Z^{b,b}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{c\theta}N,

(4.17)

provided
b>\frac{1}{2}
and close to
\frac{1}{2}
,
where
Y^{b,b}
and
Z^{b,b}
are defined in (
2.5
) and (
2.6
), respectively.

Proof.

From (
2.5
) and (
3.7
),
we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathcal{P}^{\pm}\|_{Y^{1-b,b}}\leq\bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\langle\tau\rangle^{2(1-b)}\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle^{-2b}\|\widehat{P^{\pm}_{kk^{\prime}}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}^{2}d\tau d\tau^{\prime}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim T^{\theta}L_{\max}^{-3\delta_{0}},\end{split}

(4.18)

for some
b>\frac{1}{2}
.
We recall that

\mathcal{P}^{+}[\psi](t)=-i\chi(t)\int^{t}_{0}\Pi_{N}\mathcal{M}(y_{L_{1}},y_{L_{2}},\psi)(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}.

It then follows that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathcal{P}^{+}[\psi](t)&\|_{X^{1}}\sim\|\mathcal{P}^{+}[\psi](t)\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}([-T,T]\times\mathbb{T})}+\|\partial_{t}(\mathcal{P}^{+}[\psi](t))\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}([-T,T]\times\mathbb{T})}\\
&\lesssim L_{\max}^{4}\|y_{L_{1}}\|_{X^{b}}\|y_{L_{2}}\|_{X^{b}}\|\psi\|_{X^{0}}\lesssim L_{\max}^{12}\|\psi\|_{X^{0}},\end{split}

(4.19)

where we used that
y_{L}=u_{L}-u_{L/2}
is Fourier supported in
[-L,L]
, and that
u_{L}=\sum_{L^{\prime}\leq L}y_{L^{\prime}}
satisfies
\|y_{L^{\prime}}\|_{X^{b}}\lesssim L^{\prime}
due to the induction argument.
In particular,
(
4.19
) implies that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}^{\pm}\|_{Y^{1,0}}\lesssim L_{\max}^{12},

which together with (
4.18
) implies (
4.15
) by interpolation.

We turn to the proof of (
4.16
).
By using (
2.9
) and Minkowski
inequality, it follows that

\begin{split}&\big\|\langle\tau\rangle\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle^{-b}\widehat{P_{kk^{\prime}}^{\pm}}(\tau,\tau^{\prime})\big\|_{L^{2}_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\sum_{k_{1}-k_{2}=k-k^{\prime}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\left\|\frac{1}{\langle\tau^{\prime}\rangle\langle\tau-\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}-\tau^{\prime}-\Phi\rangle}\right\|_{L^{2}_{\tau\tau^{\prime}}}|(\widehat{y_{L_{1}}})_{k_{1}}(\tau_{1})\overline{(\widehat{y_{L_{2}}})_{k_{2}}}(\tau_{2})|d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2}\\
&\quad\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\|(\widehat{y_{L_{1}}})_{k_{1}}(\tau_{1})\|_{\ell_{k_{1}}^{2}}\|\overline{(\widehat{y_{L_{2}}})_{k_{2}}}(\tau_{2})\|_{\ell_{k_{2}}^{2}}d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2}\\
&\quad\lesssim L_{\max}^{12}.\end{split}

Thus by summing above over
|k|,|k^{\prime}|\lesssim N
we arrive at

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{P}^{\pm}\|_{Z^{1,b}}\lesssim N^{2}L_{\max}^{12}.

(4.20)

Then we conclude (
4.16
) by interpolating (
3.8
) and (
4.20
).

The proof for (
4.17
) is similar; thus, we omit it.
∎

In the next two subsections, we will establish (
4.7
) and (
4.11
) for the low-low-high random matrix
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}
given in (
4.8
); and (
4.12
) and (
4.13
) for the low-low-high random matrix
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}
given in (
4.14
).

4.2.
Low-low-high random matrix

In this subsection, we focus on the low-low-high random matrix
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}
given in (
4.8
), and establish (
4.7
) and (
4.11
).
We consider (
4.8
) with input
(w_{L_{1}},w_{L_{2}})
of types (a) (C, C), (b) (C, D), (c) (D, C), (d) (D, D).

4.2.1.
Case (a)

Type (C, C).
In this case, the matrix (
4.8
) can be written as

\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\cdot\sum_{k_{1}^{\prime},k_{2}^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\frac{g_{k_{1}^{\prime}}\overline{g_{k_{2}^{\prime}}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{2}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}

where
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}=\{h_{k_{j}k^{\prime}_{j}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}\}
(
j\in\{1,2\}
) are either identity map over
L_{j}/2<\langle k_{j}\rangle\leq L_{j}
or satisfying (
4.9
).

We first consider the non-pairing case, i.e.
k^{\prime}_{1}\neq k^{\prime}_{2}
, for which we apply Proposition
2.24
followed by Proposition
2.17
to get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\lesssim(L_{1}L_{2})^{-\alpha/2}\big(\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{1}\to k_{2}k^{\prime}}+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{2}\to k_{1}k^{\prime}}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{1}k_{2}\to k^{\prime}}+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{k\to k_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big)\prod_{j=1}^{2}\|h_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}\|_{k_{j}\to k^{\prime}_{j}},\end{split}

(4.21)

which is enough for our purpose.
When
L_{1}=L_{2}
, (
4.21
) follows from Proposition
2.24
and then Proposition
2.17
as
k_{1}^{\prime}\neq k_{2}^{\prime}
.
In what follows, we only consider (
4.21
) for the case where
L_{1}\neq L_{2}
. Suppose
L_{1}<L_{2}
(the case
L_{2}<L_{1}
is similar). Then the coefficients
h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}
may not be independent of
(g_{k_{1}^{\prime}})_{L_{1}/2<\langle k_{1}^{\prime}\rangle\leq L_{1}}
. Therefore, we cannot use Proposition
2.24
to
k_{1}^{\prime}
and
k_{2}^{\prime}
directly.
To overcome this obstacle, we first use Proposition
2.24
only with respect to
k_{2}^{\prime}
and then Proposition
2.17
to get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|&\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\lesssim L_{2}^{-\alpha/2}\bigg\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\frac{g_{k_{1}^{\prime}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\bigg\|_{(kk_{2}^{\prime}\to k^{\prime})\cap(k\to k^{\prime}k_{2}^{\prime})}\\
&\hphantom{X}\lesssim L_{2}^{-\alpha/2}\bigg\|\sum_{k_{1}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\frac{g_{k_{1}^{\prime}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\bigg\|_{(kk_{2}\to k^{\prime})\cap(k\to k^{\prime}k_{2})}\|{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\|_{k_{2}\to k^{\prime}_{2}}\end{split}

(4.22)

where
\|\cdot\|_{X\cap Y}=\|\cdot\|_{X}+\|\cdot\|_{Y}
.
Then, we note that the tensor
{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}
is independent of
(g_{k_{1}^{\prime}})_{L_{1}/2<\langle k_{1}^{\prime}\rangle\leq L_{1}}
, which enables us to apply Proposition
2.24
again with respect to
k_{1}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bigg\|&\sum_{k_{1}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\sum_{k_{1}^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\frac{g_{k_{1}^{\prime}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\bigg\|_{(kk_{2}\to k^{\prime})\cap(k\to k^{\prime}k_{2})}\\
&\lesssim L_{1}^{-\alpha/2}\bigg\|\sum_{k_{1}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\bigg\|_{(kk_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}\to k^{\prime})\cap(kk_{1}^{\prime}\to k^{\prime}k_{2})\cap(kk_{2}\to k^{\prime}k_{1}^{\prime})\cap(k\to k^{\prime}k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime})}\\
&\lesssim L_{1}^{-\alpha/2}\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{(kk_{2}k_{1}\to k^{\prime})\cap(kk_{1}\to k^{\prime}k_{2})\cap(kk_{2}\to k^{\prime}k_{1})\cap(k\to k^{\prime}k_{2}k_{1})}\|h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\|_{k_{1}\to k^{\prime}_{1}}.\end{split}

(4.23)

By collecting (
4.22
) and (
4.23
), we conclude (
4.21
) for
L_{1}<L_{2}
.
By Lemma
2.13
, Lemma
2.14
, with
N_{1}=L_{1}
,
N_{2}=L_{2}
, and
N_{3}=N
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|&{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{(kk_{2}k_{1}\to k^{\prime})\cap(kk_{1}\to k^{\prime}k_{2})\cap(kk_{2}\to k^{\prime}k_{1})\cap(k\to k^{\prime}k_{2}k_{1})}\\
&=\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{1}\to k_{2}k^{\prime}}+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{2}\to k_{1}k^{\prime}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXXX}+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{1}k_{2}\to k^{\prime}}+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{k\to k_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\\
&\lesssim L_{2}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{1}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}+L_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}+L_{1}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\log L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{2}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\log L_{1})^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(4.24)

From (
4.21
), (
4.24
), and (
4.9
), we conclude that

\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\lesssim(L_{1}L_{2})^{-\alpha/2}L_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim L^{-\frac{\alpha-1}{2}}_{\max},

which is sufficient for (
4.7
) since
\alpha>1
and
\delta_{0}\ll\alpha-1
.

A similar argument as above also gives

\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{kk^{\prime}}&\lesssim(L_{1}L_{2})^{-\alpha/2}\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{2}\|h_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}\|_{k_{j}\to k^{\prime}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim(L_{1}L_{2})^{-\alpha/2}\big(N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\log L_{\max})^{\frac{1}{2}}+(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\\
&\lesssim(L_{1}L_{2})^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}N^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{\varepsilon}_{\max}\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\varepsilon}_{\max},\end{split}

where
\varepsilon\ll 1
,
L_{\min}=\min(L_{1},L_{2})
, and
L_{\max}=\max(L_{1},L_{2})
,
which gives (
4.11
).

Now we consider the pairing case, i.e.
k^{\prime}_{1}=k^{\prime}_{2}
. Note that
L_{1}=L_{2}=L
.
In this case, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}&=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\sum_{k_{1}^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{2}}}\frac{|g_{k_{1}^{\prime}}|^{2}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha}}\\
&=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\sum_{k_{1}^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{2}}}\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}+\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\sum_{k_{1}^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{2}}}\frac{|g_{k_{1}^{\prime}}|^{2}-1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha}}\\
&=:\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(1)}+\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(2)}.\end{split}

(4.25)

We first consider the term
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(1)}
.
By using Proposition
2.17
, and then Lemma
2.14
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|&\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(1)}\big\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\lesssim L^{-\alpha}\bigg\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{1}^{\prime}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{2}}}\bigg\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\\
&\lesssim L^{-\alpha}\min\big(\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{kk_{1}k_{2}\to k^{\prime}},\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{k\to k_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big)\Big\|\sum_{k_{1}^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{2}}}\Big\|_{k_{1}k_{2}}\\
&\lesssim L^{-\alpha}\min\big(\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{kk_{1}k_{2}\to k^{\prime}},\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{k^{\prime}k_{1}k_{2}\to k}\big)\|h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\|_{k_{1}\to k_{1}^{\prime}}\|{h_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{2}}}\|_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}\\
&\lesssim L^{-\alpha}\big(L^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\varepsilon}+L^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)L^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}\lesssim L^{1-\alpha+\gamma_{0}},\end{split}

(4.26)

which is sufficient for (
4.7
) provided
\gamma_{0}\ll\alpha-1
.
For the term
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(2)}
in (
4.25
),
we apply Proposition

2.24
with
\eta_{k_{1}^{\prime}}=|g_{k_{1}^{\prime}}|^{2}-1
, then Lemma
2.18
, and Lemma
2.14
, to get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|&\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(2)}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\lesssim\bigg\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}\sum_{k_{1}^{\prime}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{2}}}\frac{\eta_{k_{1}^{\prime}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha}}\bigg\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\\
&\lesssim L^{-\alpha}\bigg\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{2}}}\bigg\|_{(kk^{\prime}_{1}\to k^{\prime})\cap(k\to k^{\prime}k^{\prime}_{1})}\\
&\lesssim L^{-\alpha}\Big(\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{kk_{1}k_{2}\to k^{\prime}}+\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{k\to k_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\Big)\prod_{j=1}^{2}\|h_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}\|_{k_{j}\to k^{\prime}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim L^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha},\end{split}

(4.27)

which is sufficient for (
4.7
).

We proceed to consider the estimate (
4.11
) for the pairing case, where
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}
is given by (
4.25
).
We begin with
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(1)}
in (
4.25
). Here we have to use the cancellation that arises from the unitary property of
\widetilde{H}^{N,L}_{kk^{\prime}}
, namely Lemma
3.6
. Specifically, by Corollary
3.7
and Remark
3.8
, we can redefine
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(1)}
, keeping the same notation for
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(1)}
, as

\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(1)}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\sum_{k_{1}^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L,R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L,R_{2}}}\bigg(\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha}}\bigg),

since
k_{1}\neq k_{2}
and
L_{1}=L_{2}=L
.
From (
3.4
), we may assume that
|k_{1}-k_{1}^{\prime}|\lesssim L^{\varepsilon}R_{1}
, which implies that

\left|\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha}}-\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha}}\right|\lesssim L^{-\alpha-1+\varepsilon}R_{1}.

(4.28)

Then, a similar argument as in (
4.26
) together with (
4.28
) yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(1)}\big\|_{kk^{\prime}}&\lesssim L^{-\alpha-1+\varepsilon}R_{1}\bigg\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{1}^{\prime}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{2}}}\bigg\|_{kk^{\prime}}\\
&\lesssim L^{-\alpha-1+\varepsilon}R_{1}\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\|_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}\|{h_{k_{2}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{2}}}\|_{k_{2}\to k_{1}^{\prime}}.\end{split}

(4.29)

By Lemma
2.12
and (
4.9
), we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(1)}\big\|_{kk^{\prime}}&\lesssim L^{-\alpha-1+\varepsilon}R_{1}\big(N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}+L\big)L^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}R_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha+2\varepsilon+\gamma_{0}}+L^{1-\alpha+\varepsilon+\gamma_{0}},\end{split}

(4.30)

which is sufficient for the estimate (
4.11
).

Finally, we turn to
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(2)}
.
Proceeding as in (
4.27
),
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{(2)}\|_{kk^{\prime}}&\lesssim L^{-\alpha}\bigg\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L,R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k^{\prime}_{1}}^{L,R_{2}}}\bigg\|_{(kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}^{\prime})\cap(kk^{\prime}k_{1}^{\prime})}\\
&\lesssim L^{-\alpha}\big(\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{2}}\|h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L,R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k^{\prime}_{1}}^{L,R_{2}}}\|_{k_{1}k_{2}\to k_{1}^{\prime}}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt+\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L,R_{1}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k^{\prime}_{1}}^{L,R_{2}}}\|_{k_{1}^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{2}}\big)\\
&\lesssim L^{-\alpha}\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\prod_{j=1}^{2}\|h_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}\|_{k_{j}\to k^{\prime}_{j}}\\
&\lesssim L^{-\alpha}(N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\varepsilon}L^{\frac{1}{2}}+L)\lesssim N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}+\varepsilon}L^{\frac{1}{2}-\alpha}+L^{1-\alpha},\end{split}

(4.31)

which is again sufficient for (
4.11
).

Finally, by collecting (
4.25
), (
4.26
), (
4.27
), (
4.30
), and (
4.31
), we finish the proof of (
4.7
) and (
4.11
)
for Case (a).

4.2.2.
Case (b) and Case (c)

Type (C, D) and type (D, C).
Without loss of generality, we only consider the case when
(w_{L_{1}},w_{L_{2}})
are of type (D, C).
The random matrix (
4.8
) can be written as

\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\cdot\sum_{k_{2}^{\prime}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\frac{\overline{g_{k_{2}^{\prime}}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{2}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

where
w_{L_{1}}
satisfies (
4.10
) and
\widetilde{\mathfrak{h}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}=\{h_{k_{2}k^{\prime}_{2}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}\}
are either identity map over
L_{2}/2<\langle k_{2}\rangle\leq L_{2}
or satisfying
(
4.9
).
We apply Proposition
2.17
to get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}&\lesssim\min\bigg(\bigg\|\sum_{k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\sum_{k_{2}^{\prime}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\frac{\overline{g_{k_{2}^{\prime}}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{2}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\bigg\|_{k\to k_{1}k^{\prime}},\\
&\hphantom{XXXX,}\bigg\|\sum_{k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\sum_{k_{2}^{\prime}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\frac{\overline{g_{k_{2}^{\prime}}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{2}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\bigg\|_{kk_{1}\to k^{\prime}}\bigg)\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}.\end{split}

(4.32)

Recall that the tensor
{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}
is independent of
F_{L_{2}}
. We can apply Proposition
2.24
to (
4.32
), and then apply Proposition
2.17
to get

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}&\lesssim L_{2}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\min\bigg(\Big\|\sum_{k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\Big\|_{(kk_{2}^{\prime}\to k_{1}k^{\prime})\cap(k\to k_{1}k_{2}^{\prime}k^{\prime})},\\
&\hskip 62.59596pt\Big\|\sum_{k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\Big\|_{(kk_{1}k_{2}^{\prime}\to k^{\prime})\cap(kk_{1}\to k_{2}^{\prime}k^{\prime})}\bigg)\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}\\
&\lesssim L_{2}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big\|{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\big\|_{k_{2}\to k_{2}^{\prime}}\min\Big(\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big\|_{(kk_{2}\to k_{1}k^{\prime})\cap(k\to k_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime})},\\
&\hskip 62.59596pt\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big\|_{(kk_{1}k_{2}\to k^{\prime})\cap(kk_{1}\to k_{2}k^{\prime})}\Big)\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}\\
&\lesssim L_{2}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big\|{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\big\|_{k_{2}\to k_{2}^{\prime}}\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big\|_{(kk_{1}k_{2}\to k^{\prime})\cap(kk_{1}\to k_{2}k^{\prime})}\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}.\end{split}

(4.33)

By Lemma
2.13
and Lemma
2.14
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}&\big\|_{(kk_{1}k_{2}\to k^{\prime})\cap(kk_{1}\to k_{2}k^{\prime})}=\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big\|_{kk_{1}k_{2}\to k^{\prime}}+\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big\|_{kk_{1}\to k_{2}k^{\prime}}\\
&\lesssim L_{1}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\log L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{1}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{2}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\lesssim L_{1}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

which together with (
4.33
), (
4.9
), and (
4.10
), implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}&\lesssim L_{2}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big\|{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\big\|_{k_{2}\to k_{2}^{\prime}}L_{1}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}\\
&\lesssim L_{1}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\lesssim(L_{1}L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}},\end{split}

which proves (
4.7
) since
\delta_{0}\ll\alpha-1
.

Now we turn to the estimate (
4.11
). Similar computation as in (
4.32
) and (
4.33
) yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{kk^{\prime}}&\lesssim\min\bigg(\bigg\|\sum_{k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\sum_{k_{2}^{\prime}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\frac{\overline{g_{k_{2}^{\prime}}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{2}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\bigg\|_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}},\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}\bigg\|\sum_{k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\sum_{k_{2}^{\prime}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\frac{\overline{g_{k_{2}^{\prime}}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{2}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}\bigg\|_{kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}}\bigg)\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}\\
&\lesssim L_{2}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\min\bigg(\Big\|\sum_{k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\Big\|_{(kk_{1}k_{2}^{\prime}k^{\prime})\cap(kk_{1}k^{\prime}\to k_{2}^{\prime})},\\
&\hskip 62.59596pt\Big\|\sum_{k_{2}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\overline{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\Big\|_{(kk_{2}^{\prime}k^{\prime}\to k_{1})\cap(kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{2}^{\prime})}\bigg)\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}\\
&\lesssim L_{2}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big\|{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\big\|_{k_{2}\to k_{2}^{\prime}}\min\Big(\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big\|_{(kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime})\cap(kk_{1}k^{\prime}\to k_{2})},\\
&\hskip 62.59596pt\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big\|_{(kk_{2}k^{\prime}\to k_{1})\cap(kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{2})}\Big)\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}\\
&\lesssim L_{2}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big\|{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\big\|_{k_{2}\to k_{2}^{\prime}}\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big\|_{(kk_{2}k^{\prime}\to k_{1})\cap(kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{2})}\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}.\end{split}

(4.34)

By Lemma
2.13
and Lemma
2.14
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}&\big\|_{(kk_{2}k^{\prime}\to k_{1})\cap(kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{2})}=\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big\|_{kk_{2}k^{\prime}\to k_{1}}+\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\big\|_{kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{2}}\\
&\lesssim N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\log L_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{\min}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\\
&\lesssim N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{2}^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2}}+L_{\min}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}.\end{split}

(4.35)

By collecting (
4.33
), (
4.9
), (
4.10
), and (
4.35
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{kk^{\prime}}&\lesssim L_{2}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big\|{h_{k_{2}k_{2}^{\prime}}^{L_{2},R_{2}}}\big\|_{k_{2}\to k_{2}^{\prime}}\big(N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{2}^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2}}+L_{\min}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big)\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}\\
&\lesssim\big(N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{2}^{\frac{\alpha-1}{2}}+L_{\min}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big)L_{2}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}\\
&\lesssim N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}}L_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}+N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{\min}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{2}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}L^{{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}}_{\max},\end{split}

(4.36)

which is sufficient for (
4.11
).

Therefore, we finish the proof of (
4.7
) and (
4.11
)
for Case (b): type (C, D). The proof for Case (c): type (D, C) follows similarly.

4.2.3.
Case (d)

Type: (D, D).
In this case, the matrix (
4.7
) can be written as

\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}}\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\cdot(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\cdot\overline{(w_{L_{2}})_{k_{2}}},

where
w_{L_{1}}
and
w_{L_{2}}
satisfy (
4.10
).
By using
Proposition
2.17
, followed by Lemma

2.13
and (
4.10
), we obtain

\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}&\lesssim\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|_{kk_{1}\to k_{2}k^{\prime}}\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{k_{1}}\|\overline{(w_{L_{2}})_{k_{2}}}\|_{k_{2}}\\
&\lesssim(L_{1}L_{2})^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(L_{1}L_{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}\\
&\lesssim L^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}-\gamma}\lesssim L^{-4\delta_{0}},\end{split}

which is sufficient for (
4.7
), provided
\delta_{0}\ll\alpha-1
.

Similarly, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{+}\|_{kk^{\prime}}&\lesssim\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k^{\prime}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{2}}\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{k_{1}}\|\overline{(w_{L_{2}})_{k_{2}}}\|_{k_{2}}\\
&\lesssim N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{\min}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(L_{1}L_{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}\\
&\lesssim N^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}L_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}-\gamma},\end{split}

which again proves (
4.11
).

Therefore,we have finished the proof of (
4.7
) and (
4.8
)
for Case (d).

4.3.
Low-high-low random averaging operator

In this subsection, we consider the low-low-high random matrix
\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}
defined in (
4.14
), and prove (
4.12
) and (
4.13
).
We follow a similar strategy as in the previous subsection, and consider four cases for (
4.14
) depending on whether
(w_{L_{1}},w_{L_{3}})
are of types (a) (C, C), (b) (C, D), (c) (D, C), (d) (D, D).

4.3.1.
Case (a)

Type (C, C).
In this case, the matrix (
4.14
) can be written as

\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\sum_{k_{1}^{\prime},k_{3}^{\prime}}h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}{h_{k_{3}k_{3}^{\prime}}^{L_{3},R_{3}}}\frac{g_{k_{1}^{\prime}}{g_{k_{3}^{\prime}}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{1}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{3}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}}

where
\mathfrak{h}^{L_{j},R_{j}}=\{h_{k_{j}k^{\prime}_{j}}^{L_{j},R_{j}}\}
(
j\in\{1,3\}
) are either identity map over
L_{j}/2<\langle k_{j}^{\prime}\rangle\leq L_{j}
or satisfying (
4.9
).
Similar argument as in Subsection
4.2.1
yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\lesssim(L_{1}L_{3})^{-\alpha/2}\big(\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{1}\to k^{\prime}k_{3}}+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{k\to k_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\\
&\hskip 71.13188pt+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{1}k_{3}\to k^{\prime}}+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{3}\to k_{1}k^{\prime}}\big)\\
&\hskip 36.98866pt\times\|h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\|_{k_{1}\to k^{\prime}_{1}}\|{h_{k_{3}k_{3}^{\prime}}^{L_{3},R_{3}}}\|_{k_{3}\to k^{\prime}_{3}}.\end{split}

(4.37)

By using Lemma
2.13
, Lemma
2.14
, with
N_{1}=L_{1}
,
N_{2}\sim N
, and
N_{3}=L_{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|&\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{1}\to k^{\prime}k_{3}}+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{k\to k_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{1}k_{3}\to k^{\prime}}+\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk_{3}\to k_{1}k^{\prime}}\\
&\lesssim(L_{1}L_{3})^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}+L_{\max}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\log L_{\min})^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim(L_{1}L_{3})^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

(4.38)

where
L_{\min}=\min\{L_{1},L_{3}\}
and
L_{\max}=\max\{L_{1},L_{3}\}
.
Thus it follows from (
4.37
), (
4.38
), and (
4.9
), that

\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\lesssim(L_{1}L_{3})^{-\alpha/2}L_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim L^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}_{\max},

which is sufficient for (
4.12
).
A similar argument also gives

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}\|_{kk^{\prime}}&\lesssim(L_{1}L_{3})^{-\alpha/2}\big(\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\|_{kk^{\prime}k_{3}\to k_{1}}+\|\mathrm{T}^{\text{b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\|_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}\to k_{3}}\\
&\hskip 20.00003pt+\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\|_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}+\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\|_{kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{3}}\big)\\
&\hskip 10.00002pt\times\|h_{k_{1}k_{1}^{\prime}}^{L_{1},R_{1}}\|_{k_{1}\to k^{\prime}_{1}}\|h_{k_{3}k_{3}^{\prime}}^{L_{3},R_{3}}\|_{k_{3}\to k^{\prime}_{3}}.\end{split}

(4.39)

By using Lemma
2.13
, Lemma
2.14
, with
N_{1}=L_{1}
,
N_{2}=N
, and
N_{3}=L_{3}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|&{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\|_{kk^{\prime}k_{3}\to k_{1}}+\|\mathrm{T}^{\text{b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\|_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}\to k_{3}}+\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\|_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}+\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\|_{kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{3}}\\
&\lesssim L_{3}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\log N)^{\frac{1}{2}}+N^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{1}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\log N)^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{\max}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(L_{\min}\log N)^{\frac{1}{2}}+(L_{\min}N)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

(4.40)

By collecting (
4.39
) and (
4.40
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}\|_{kk^{\prime}}&\lesssim(L_{1}L_{3})^{-\alpha/2}N^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}},\end{split}

from which by choosing
\gamma_{0}\ll\alpha-1
we finish the proof of (
4.13
).

4.3.2.
Case (b) and Case (c)

Type (C, D) and type (D, C).
Without loss of generality, we only consider
(w_{L_{1}},w_{L_{3}})
of type (D, C). Thus, the random matrix (
4.14
) can be written as

\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}}{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\cdot\sum_{k_{3}^{\prime}}{h_{k_{3}k_{3}^{\prime}}^{L_{3},R_{3}}}\frac{{g_{k_{3}^{\prime}}}}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{3}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}},

where
w_{L_{1}}
satisfies (
4.10
) and
\mathfrak{h}^{L_{3},R_{3}}=\{h_{k_{3}k^{\prime}_{3}}^{L_{3},R_{3}}\}
are either identity map over
L_{3}/2<\langle k_{3}^{\prime}\rangle\leq L_{3}
or satisfying
(
4.9
).
Similar argument as in Subsection
4.2.2
yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}&\lesssim L_{3}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big\|{h_{k_{3}k_{3}^{\prime}}^{L_{3},R_{3}}}\big\|_{k_{3}\to k_{3}^{\prime}}\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\big\|_{(kk_{1}k_{3}\to k^{\prime})\cap(kk_{1}\to k_{3}k^{\prime})}\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}\\
&\lesssim L_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}L_{3}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big(L_{\max}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\log L_{\min})^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}}+(L_{1}L_{3})^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big)\\
&\lesssim L_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}L_{3}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(L_{1}L_{3})^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\lesssim L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{2}-\gamma},\end{split}

which is again sufficient for (
4.12
) provided
\delta_{0}\ll\alpha-1
.

Similar computation as in (
4.34
), (
4.35
), and (
4.36
) yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}\|_{kk^{\prime}}&\lesssim L_{3}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big\|{h_{k_{3}k_{3}^{\prime}}^{L_{3},R_{3}}}\big\|_{k_{3}\to k_{3}^{\prime}}\big\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\big\|_{(kk^{\prime}k_{3}\to k_{1})\cap(kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{3})}\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{\ell^{2}_{k_{1}}}\\
&\lesssim L_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}L_{3}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\big(L_{3}^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\log N)^{\frac{1}{2}}+N^{\frac{1}{2}}+L_{\min}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}N^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}\big)\\
&\lesssim N^{\varepsilon}L_{1}^{-\gamma-\frac{1}{2}}L_{3}^{1-\alpha}+N^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}L_{3}^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}+N^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}L_{1}^{-\gamma-\frac{\alpha}{4}}L_{3}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3\alpha}{4}}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma},\end{split}

which is sufficient for (
4.13
) as
\gamma_{0}\ll\gamma
. Here we used that
\alpha\in(1,2)
.

Therefore, we finish the proof of (
4.12
) and (
4.13
)
for Case (b): type (C, D). The proof for Case (c): type (D, C) follows similarly.

4.3.3.
Case (d)

Type: (D, D).
In this case, the matrix (
4.14
) can be written as

\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{3}}\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\cdot(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\cdot\left({w_{L_{3}}}\right)_{k_{3}},

where
w_{L_{1}}
and
w_{L_{3}}
satisfy (
4.10
).
By using
Proposition
2.17
, followed by Lemma

2.13
and (
4.10
), we obtain

\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}&\|_{k\to k^{\prime}}\lesssim\|{\rm T}^{{\rm b},m}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}\|_{kk_{1}\to k^{\prime}k_{3}}\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{k_{1}}\|({w_{L_{3}}})_{k_{3}}\|_{k_{3}}\\
&\lesssim(L_{1}L_{3})^{1-\frac{\alpha}{2}}(L_{1}L_{3})^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}\lesssim L_{\max}^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}-\gamma},\end{split}

which is sufficient for (
4.7
).

Similarly, we have

\begin{split}\|\mathscr{Y}_{k,k^{\prime}}^{-}\|_{kk^{\prime}}&\lesssim\|\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k^{\prime}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\|_{kk^{\prime}\to k_{1}k_{3}}\|(w_{L_{1}})_{k_{1}}\|_{k_{1}}\|({w_{L_{3}}})_{k_{3}}\|_{k_{3}}\\
&\lesssim(NL_{\min})^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}(L_{1}L_{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}\\
&\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}}L_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma}\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}}L_{\max}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}-\gamma},\end{split}

where we used that
N\geq L_{\max}
, which proves (
4.13
).

Therefore,we have finished the proof of (
4.12
) and (
4.14
)
for Case (d): type (D, D).

4.4.
Random averaging operator

The main purpose of this subsection is to
prove (
3.3
) and (
3.4
) in
\text{{{Local}}}(M)
by
assuming
\text{{{Local}}}\left(\frac{M}{2}\right)
.
Let

\mathfrak{p}^{N,L}=\mathcal{P}^{N,L}-\mathcal{P}^{N,L/2},

(4.41)

where
\mathcal{P}^{N,L}
be the
linear operator defined in (
1.33
).
Recall from
(
1.35
) and (
4.41
) that

\mathcal{H}^{N,L}=\Pi_{N}(1-\mathcal{P}^{N,L})^{-1}=\Pi_{N}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(\mathcal{P}^{N,L})^{n},

(4.42)

and thus the operator
\mathfrak{h}^{N,L}
in (
1.37
) has the following expansion,

\mathfrak{h}^{N,L}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}(-1)^{n-1}(\mathcal{H}^{N,L}\mathfrak{p}^{N,L})^{n}\mathcal{H}^{N,L}.

(4.43)

Recalling the operator
\mathcal{P}^{+}:=\mathcal{P}^{+}_{N,L_{1},L_{2}}
from (
3.6
), we have

\begin{split}\mathcal{P}^{N,L}[\psi]&=-2i\chi(t)\int_{0}^{t}\Pi_{N}\mathcal{M}(u_{L},u_{L},\psi)(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}\\
&=-2i\sum_{L_{1},L_{2}\leq L}\chi(t)\int_{0}^{t}\Pi_{N}\mathcal{M}(y_{L_{1}},y_{L_{2}},\psi)(t^{\prime})dt^{\prime}\\
&=\mathcal{P}^{N,\frac{L}{2}}\psi+2\sum_{L_{\max}=L}\mathcal{P}_{N,L_{1},L_{2}}^{+}\psi,\end{split}

(4.44)

where
\chi(t)
is the time localization given in (
1.20
).
Recall that
\mathcal{P}_{N,L_{1},L_{2}}^{+}
satisfies the estimates
(
3.7
)-(
3.8
),
which implies that
\mathfrak{p}^{N,L}
also satisfies the estimates (
3.7
) and (
3.8
) but with a logarithm loss coming from the summation over
L_{i}
.
This logarithm loss is harmless since it can be absorbed.
Namely, from (
4.41
), (
4.44
), and Corollary
4.1
, we have

\begin{cases}\|\mathfrak{p}^{N,L}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\lesssim T^{c\theta}L^{-2\delta_{0}}(\log L)^{2};\\
\|\mathfrak{p}^{N,L}\|_{Z^{b,b}}\lesssim T^{c\theta}N^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2\gamma_{0}}{3}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}-\gamma_{0}}(\log L)^{2},\end{cases}

(4.45)

which together with (
4.41
) implies

\begin{cases}\|\mathcal{P}^{N,L}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\lesssim T^{c\theta},\\
\|\mathcal{P}^{N,L}\|_{Z^{b,b}}\lesssim T^{c\theta}N^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{2\gamma_{0}}{3}}\end{cases}

(4.46)

for some
c>0
.

From the definition of the operator norm, we note that

\|\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\|_{Y^{b,b}}=\|\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\leq\|\mathcal{A}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\|\mathcal{B}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}

(4.47)

which together with (
4.42
) and (
4.46
) implies that

\begin{split}\|\mathcal{H}^{N,L}-\Pi_{N}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}&\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\|(\mathcal{P}^{N,L})^{n}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\|\mathcal{P}^{N,L}\|^{n}_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\\
&\leq\|\mathcal{P}^{N,L}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\sum_{n\geq 1}(CT^{c\theta})^{n-1}\lesssim T^{c\theta}.\end{split}

(4.48)

Also, note that
\|\Pi_{N}\|_{X^{b}(J)\to X^{b}(J)}\lesssim 1
.
In particular, we conclude from (
4.48
) that

\|\mathcal{H}^{N,L}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\lesssim 1,

which together with (
4.43
), (
4.47
), and (
4.45
), implies

\begin{split}\|\mathfrak{h}^{N,L}&\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\|(\mathcal{H}^{N,L}\mathfrak{p}^{N,L})^{n}\mathcal{H}^{N,L}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\|\mathcal{H}^{N,L}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}^{n+1}\|\mathfrak{p}^{N,L}\|^{n}_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\\
&\leq\|\mathfrak{p}^{N,L}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}C^{n+1}(T^{c\theta}L^{-\delta_{0}})^{n-1}\lesssim T^{c\theta}L^{-\delta_{0}},\end{split}

which gives the first bound in (
3.3
) by choosing
T\ll 1
.

We also note that

\|\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}\|_{Z^{b,b}}\leq\min\big(\|\mathcal{A}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\|\mathcal{B}\|_{Z^{b,b}},\|\mathcal{A}\|_{Z^{b,b}}\|\mathcal{B}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\big),

which together with (
4.43
) and (
4.46
) implies

\begin{split}\|\mathfrak{h}^{N,L}\|_{Z^{b,b}}&\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\|(\mathcal{H}^{N,L}\mathfrak{p}^{N,L})^{n}\mathcal{H}^{N,L}\|_{Z^{b,b}}\\
&\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\|\mathcal{H}^{N,L}\|^{n+1}_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\|\mathfrak{p}^{N,L}\|^{n-1}_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\|\mathfrak{p}^{N,L}\|_{Z^{b,b}}\\
&\leq\|\mathfrak{p}^{N,L}\|_{Z^{b,b}}\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}C^{n+1}(T^{c\theta}L^{-\delta_{0}})^{n-1}\\
&\leq T^{c\theta}N^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}L^{-\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

provided
T\ll 1
,
which completes the proof of the second bound of (
3.3
).

Finally, from Proposition
2.19
, we have

\bigg\|\bigg(1+\frac{|k-k^{\prime}|}{L}\bigg)^{\kappa}(\widehat{\mathcal{A}\mathcal{B}})_{kk^{\prime}}\bigg\|_{Z^{b,b}}\leq\|\mathcal{A}\|_{X^{b}\to X^{b}}\bigg\|\bigg(1+\frac{|k-k^{\prime}|}{L}\bigg)^{\kappa}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{kk^{\prime}}\bigg\|_{Z^{b,b}},

which, together with a similar argument as above, gives

\bigg\|\bigg(1+\frac{|k-k^{\prime}|}{L}\bigg)^{\kappa}h^{N,L}_{kk^{\prime}}\bigg\|_{Z^{b,b}}\leq T^{c\theta}N,

provided
T\ll 1
, which proves (
3.4
).

5.1.
Preparation of the proof

We first see that

\|\mathscr{A}(w_{N_{1}},w_{N_{2}},w_{N_{3}})\|_{X^{1}}\lesssim\|\mathcal{M}(w_{N_{1}},w_{N_{2}},w_{N_{3}})\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\lesssim{{M^{2}}}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\|w_{N_{i}}\|_{X^{b}}\lesssim M^{14}.

Therefore, to prove (
5.4
), from interpolation, it suffices to show

\|\mathscr{A}(w_{N_{1}},w_{N_{2}},w_{N_{3}})\|_{X^{1-b}}\lesssim M^{-\frac{1}{2}{{-2\gamma}}},

(5.6)

provided
b-\frac{1}{2}\ll\gamma_{0}
. By (
1.22
),
(
2.8
), and (
5.2
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathcal{F}_{t,x}(\mathscr{A}(w_{N_{1}},w_{N_{2}},w_{N_{3}}))(\tau,k)\\
&\quad={{-i}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1}-k_{2}+k_{3}=k\\
k_{2}\not\in\{k_{1},k_{3}\}\end{subarray}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{K}(\tau,\Phi+\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}+\tau_{3})\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt\times(\widehat{w_{N_{1}}})_{k_{1}}(\tau_{1})\cdot\overline{(\widehat{w_{N_{2}}})_{k_{2}}}(\tau_{2})\cdot(\widehat{w_{N_{3}}})_{k_{3}}(\tau_{3})d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2}d\tau_{3}\\
&={{-i}}\sum_{m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}\mathcal{K}(\tau,m+\Phi-[\Phi]+\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}+\tau_{3})\times\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\\
&\hskip 42.67912pt\times(\widehat{w_{N_{1}}})_{k_{1}}(\tau_{1})\cdot\overline{(\widehat{w_{N_{2}}})_{k_{2}}}(\tau_{2})\cdot\left(\widehat{w_{N_{3}}}\right)_{k_{3}}(\tau_{3})d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2}d\tau_{3},\end{split}

(5.7)

where
\Phi
is defined in (
1.23
),
[\Phi]
is the integer part of
\Phi
, and the tensor
\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}
is the base tensor given in (
2.44
).
We note that
\Phi-[\Phi]=O(1)
.
Then from (
5.7
), (
2.9
), and Minkowski inequality, we obtain

\begin{split}&\|\mathscr{A}(w_{N_{1}},w_{N_{2}},w_{N_{3}})\|_{X^{1-b}}^{2}\\
&\quad\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle\tau\rangle^{-2b}\bigg(\sum_{m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bigg\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}\langle\tau-m+O(1)-\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}-\tau_{3}\rangle^{-1}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\times\langle\tau_{1}\rangle^{-b}\langle\tau_{2}\rangle^{-b}\langle\tau_{3}\rangle^{-b}\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\langle\tau_{i}\rangle^{b}(\widehat{w_{N_{i}}})^{\zeta_{i}}_{k_{i}}(\tau_{i})\bigg\|_{\ell_{k}^{2}}d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2}d\tau_{3}\bigg)^{2}d\tau\\
&\quad\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}}\langle\tau\rangle^{-2b}\bigg(\sum_{m}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\langle\tau-m-\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}-\tau_{3}\rangle^{-1}\langle\tau_{1}\rangle^{-b}\langle\tau_{2}\rangle^{-b}\langle\tau_{3}\rangle^{-b}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\times\bigg\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\langle\tau_{i}\rangle^{b}(\widehat{w_{N_{i}}})^{\zeta_{i}}_{k_{i}}(\tau_{i})\bigg\|_{\ell_{k}^{2}}d\tau_{1}d\tau_{2}d\tau_{3}\bigg)^{2}d\tau,\end{split}

where
\zeta_{1},\zeta_{3}=+
and
\zeta_{2}=-
.
Due to the fact that
|{k_{1}}|^{\alpha}-|{k_{2}}|^{\alpha}+|{k_{3}}|^{\alpha}-|{k}|^{\alpha}-O(1)=m
, we see that
|m|\lesssim N^{\alpha}
, which leads to

\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\langle\tau-m-\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}-\tau_{3}\rangle^{-1}\lesssim 1+\log N.

Therefore, to
prove (
5.6
) it only needs to show

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bigg\|\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\prod_{i=1}^{3}\langle\tau_{i}\rangle^{b}(\widehat{w_{N_{i}}})^{\zeta_{i}}_{k_{i}}(\tau_{i})\bigg\|_{L^{2}_{\tau_{1}\tau_{2}\tau_{3}}\ell_{k}^{2}}\lesssim M^{-\frac{1}{2}-3\gamma},\end{split}

(5.8)

where
w_{N_{i}}
are of types (C) or (D).
In view of (
3.18
) and (
3.19
), we may further reduce (
5.8
) to the following estimate,

\|\mathscr{X}_{k}\|_{\ell_{k}^{2}}\lesssim M^{-\frac{1}{2}-3\gamma},

(5.9)

where
\mathscr{X}_{k}
is given by

\mathscr{X}_{k}=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}\mathrm{T}_{kk_{1}k_{2}k_{3}}^{\text{b},m}\cdot(w_{N_{1}})_{k_{1}}\cdot\overline{(w_{N_{2}})_{k_{2}}}\cdot(w_{N_{3}})_{k_{3}}

(5.10)

and
w_{N_{j}}
are either of the following two types (with a slight abuse of notation, we still call them type (C) and (D)).

•

Type (C), where

(w_{N_{j}})_{k_{j}}=\sum_{k_{j}^{\prime}}h_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{N_{j},L_{j}}\frac{g_{k_{j}^{\prime}}(\omega)}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[k_{j}^{\prime}]\hskip-1.70717pt]^{\alpha/2}},

with

\mathcal{B}_{\leq L_{j}}
-measurable for some

&\|h^{N_{j},L_{j}}_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}\|_{\ell_{k_{j}k_{j}^{\prime}}^{2}}\lesssim N^{\frac{1}{2}+\gamma_{0}}_{j}L_{j}^{-\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

for

(5.12)

We will now analyze the terms (I)-(VII) in the expression of

5.2.
High-high interaction

In this subsection, we focus on the scenarios where

for a small positive constant

. In particular, these scenarios cover terms (I) and (III) from the previous section.
As we have seen before, the base tensor
